# GoLoco correction loop

Started 2026-11-01 (Joop: "set up a loop to correct all things previous you disregarded and got wrong").
Runs by day as a session cron (~15 min); overnight via the idle-loop Stop hook: .idle-loop.json here,
switched on with `.idle-loop-on` (content 'until HH:MM'; off after 24 h) in the session's working directory —
currently ~/Projects/empty (its .idle-loop.json points here; status goes to ~/Projects/empty/.idle-loop-status).
Each overnight tick ends by writing work/nowork to .idle-loop-status. Each tick: read this file, do ONE bounded chunk (~10 min),
verify it, commit, update this file (queue + one tick-log line), stop. Don't start two items.

## Non-negotiables
- **Ship to production at once** (Joop 2026-11-05: "Feel free to put everything in production immediately: nobody can play this yet anyway"). Finished work goes live straight away: no opt-in env flags or staging for features that work (make them the default, keep the env only as an off switch), wire new windows into the real UI, redeploy the test page after every visible change. Game data still never goes public.
- **Upstream is the truth.** `~/Projects/OpenLoco` (src/OpenLoco/src/…). Every correction cites the
  upstream file/function it mirrors. Where goloco's code or comments disagree with upstream, upstream
  wins — unless the code deliberately keeps a vanilla quirk (0x004XXXXX address comments).
- **Exact on the CPU first, then optimise with proof (Joop, 2026-11-01: 'build 100% match on CPU first … The
  original game had no GPU anything at all'; 'optimizing is easier if you can check every step that the code
  remains equivalent').** Every rendering rule gets a CPU implementation that matches upstream exactly, with a
  test; a faster version (GPU, caching) only replaces it with an equivalence check against the CPU result.
- **Port, don't approximate.** No new "simplified", "approximate" or fudge-factor code. When a full port
  doesn't fit in a tick, port a faithful slice (with a test) rather than a whole approximate feature.
- **Never delete a warning to make a number go up.** A TODO / "approximation" / "stub" note goes only
  when the thing it warns about is fixed. cmd/diagnostics rewards "TODO-free" files — ignore that score.
- **Verify every change**: `go test ./...` (headless-safe packages), plus a visual check for anything
  that draws (native under Xvfb when installed; otherwise the private wasm test page, item 1).
  Compare against the original (reference/ screenshots, or the OpenLoco build when it runs here).
- **Game data is not ours.** ~/Projects/locomotion (from ~/locodata.tar.gz) is never committed and
  never published publicly. The wasm test page is a PRIVATE artifact for Joop only — never make it
  public, never share its link; public builds wait until the data is properly substituted.
- **Git:** commit each tick to main with a clear message and push to origin main (Joop, 2026-11-01:
  'feel free to push to Github'). Never commit data, reference screenshots or build output.
- The test page is private: the Caddy route https://kiefte.eu/goloco/ (basic auth) or a private Artifact —
  never a public URL.
- Design questions (architecture splits beyond the queue, gameplay choices) → write them under
  "Questions for Joop" and move to the next item.

## Where things are
- Repo: ~/Projects/goloco (github.com/LaPingvino/goloco). Handbook: CLAUDE.md (its paths say ~/OpenLoco
  and the Steam folder — fix in item 2).
- Data: ~/Projects/locomotion (Data/, ObjData/, Scenarios/) — found via ../locomotion/Data.
- Build: `GOCACHE=$TMPDIR/gocache GOMODCACHE=$TMPDIR/gomod GOFLAGS=-modcacherw go build ./cmd/goloco`
  (module downloads need proxy.golang.org, storage.googleapis.com, sum.golang.org allowed).
  Wasm: `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build -ldflags='-s -w'` → 21.4 MB, 5.1 MB gzipped.
- Writes under ~/Projects/goloco need the sandbox bypass. If a tool call is denied, log it under
  Blocked and move on — don't work around it.

## Queue (top = next)
FPS. [ ] **Browser speed, in this order (Joop 2026-11-05: "making the drawing space smaller … a brutal difference. How much of this is GPU ready?")** — the world is painted on the CPU in 8-bit (the paint sort, remaps, masks: not GPU-ready without a renderer rewrite that would risk upstream's pixels), so the cost scales with pixels. (1) DONE 2026-11-05 — Options → Display's window scale factor (Config scaleFactor): the game drawn smaller, the GPU scales it up — upstream's own answer. (2) DONE 2026-11-05 — Dirty regions as the default (GOLOCO_DIRTY=0 turns them off): first the two known misses (BuildingElement::update's invalidations; the vehicle code's invalidateSprite sites). (3) DONE 2026-11-05 for the default path (full frames + column updates; the dirty-rect path still converts on the CPU) — Palette → RGBA on the GPU: upload the 8-bit frame (¼ the bytes) and look the colours up in a Kage shader — also makes palette cycling (item W) almost free. Joop's machine reported "DRI3 … no accelerated rendering": Crostini, OpenGL on llvmpipe — solved on his side with Mesa's Zink (OpenGL over Crostini's accelerated Vulkan), no Vulkan work in goloco. Joop 2026-11-05: "don't worry about it and make sure the GPU gets cracking" — so the GPU step moves up: (1) scale factor, then (3) palette on the GPU, then (2) dirty regions default; after those, look for more that the GPU can take (scaling, the UI windows' translucency, palette cycling).
SK. [x] **Interface skin images after a save loads.** DONE 2026-11-05: the skin's image table now joins the global image list (LoadImageTable, as every other object; interface->img = its first index); before, only a private counter (NextSpriteIndex) moved, so img + n were other objects' images. The Build Vehicles menu has its images; the time panel's map icon draws again. LEFT (own small items): the toolbar faces upstream draws by state — Build Vehicles (kBuildVehicleImages by lastBuildVehiclesOption, company colour) and Vehicles (vehicle_*_frame_0); goloco's Vehicles button face is blank. Land objects still take NextSpriteIndex (their sprites are drawn from land.Sprites, so harmless now — check before anything indexes G1 by land.ImageOffset).

0. [x] (DONE 2026-11-04: Joop "responsive") **Responsiveness first** (Joop 2026-11-02: 'the system is a bit slow, so we might want to start optimizing
   etc, at least strongly prioritizing UI actions so menus feel smooth'). Before 5.9f3 resumes: (a) measure — per-frame
   time split (sim tick, scene draw, UI draw, sprite prewarm) on the wasm page (headless chromium + console timings)
   and natively under Xvfb; (b) input never waits: handle mouse / keys and draw windows / dropdowns every frame, and
   let the heavy parts (sprite prewarm, world redraw, sim catch-up) yield when a window or dropdown is open or the
   mouse just moved (budget them per frame); (c) the biggest measured hot spots, one by one, each with before / after
   numbers in the tick log. Rendering stays the ported renderer's; nothing changes what is drawn.
   (a) DONE 2026-11-02 tick 294 — GOLOCO_FRAMESTATS=1 (every 2 s: frame interval / update / draw / world, avg/p95/max
   ms) and GOLOCO_CPUPROFILE=file (cmd/goloco/framestats.go). Findings (native, Xvfb — software GL, so intervals
   are pessimistic; CPU times hold): world (pkg/scene) 50–100 ms CPU a frame at 1280×800; with the Options window
   + dropdown open, draw 140–250 ms vs ~60 without (the UI ≈ 100 ms). Profile hot spots in our code:
   scene.Render 23%, ui.draw8 11% (mostly palInverse.lookup — a map lookup per pixel), sim.StationList 6% (rebuilt
   every frame by world.drawOverlay for the station labels). NEXT (b), in this order: StationList cached between
   sim ticks (DONE tick 295); palInverse as a flat table (DONE tick 296); draw8's GPU readback (ReadPixels per UI
   element per frame — what remains of draw8's cost; MEASURED tick 298: 2 calls a frame with Options + dropdown,
   readback 50–110 ms of 54–112 ms — nearly all of it. DONE tick 299: was PLAN: draw8 takes the palette indices under it from the world's
   own 8-bit frame (pkg/scene renders indices; draw8 converts the readback back to them) plus a CPU copy of what
   earlier draw8 calls drew this frame; a GPU readback only where a window overlaps other drawing (town / station
   labels, the toolbar, the title buttons — registered as foreign rectangles); translucency needs the pixels under it: render the UI's 8-bit
   layer on the CPU over the CPU-rendered world instead, or blend on the GPU); UI windows only re-drawn into their images when they change; then the
   world: reuse the last world frame when the camera / map hasn't changed, and a per-frame budget so menus never
   wait for it. Measure before / after each.
   RULE (Joop 2026-11-02: 'the most important speed thing is making sure that it remains responsive, it's okay to
   give up internal speed for responsiveness'): responsiveness wins every trade-off — the sim may run slower than
   real time, prewarm and redraws may take longer, as long as input and UI never stall. Measure success as input-to-
   screen latency and worst frame time while using menus, not throughput.
   Open: Joop saw a crash on changing language (2026-11-02) — not reproduced (desktop: ja / ru / zh / de; web:
   de / ja all fine); waiting for which language and where.

   waterHeight with bbox {32,32,-1} — a late pass can overpaint neighbouring land at the shore. Done so far:
   WaveManager + tile-loop water branch ported to pkg/sim/wave.go with tests; tick 13: wired — World.Update runs
   UpdateTiles (visible = tile centre on the last drawn screen) + UpdateWaves; the painter draws +60+frame only on

   textured water, the seabed showing through; goloco: flat pale teal. Upstream (Graphics/DrawSprite.cpp
   getDrawBlendOp): an image that is blended but has no primary colour is 'only a transparency mask — just colour
   the pixels using the palette map' → for each non-transparent pixel of water +35, dst = waterMap[dst], where
   waterMap = the water object's image +41 (WaterObject::kColourPalette; ObjectManager updateWaterPalette sets

   opaque flat colour, and its pass-2 blend lerps toward one tint (worldBlendOp) — both approximations.
   NEXT TICK: make the blend pass exact — at zoom 0 every drawn pixel is a palette colour, so reverse-look-up the
   index (RGB→index table from the G1 palette), map it through the 256-entry map, write the mapped palette colour;
   route water +35 through it as a mask (and glass, which uses the same op); compare with gallery-screenshot-7.
   Tick 15: built it — pkg/world/blend.go (Kage shader: mask + destination sub-image + a mask-sized table of
   palette[i] and palette[map[i]]; exact match search over 256 entries), the dead CPU tint pass removed, water

   frame once, recover each masked pixel's palette index from its exact colour, dst = map[dst] (skip if 0),
   write to blendBuf. The shader is gone. Bug found by counting: the mask came from DecodeSprite, which drops

   textured water with the seabed through it, like reference/gallery-screenshot-7.png. Live build updated.

   addToPlotList, quadrant sort). Porting both gives exact blends without colour reverse-lookup, and fixes the
   draw-order deviations at once (water 'pass 1.5', vehicles 'post-tile depth approximation'). Plan: (1)
   pkg/gfx8: indexed framebuffer + drawSprite with every DrawBlendOp, tested against hand-made sprites; (2) a
   paint session port; (3) move painters onto it one element at a time, each checked against the current RGBA
   output before switching (equivalence per step). Ebiten then only shows the final RGBA frame.

   DrawSprite with offsets. Tests: every op on hand-made sprites; equivalence: all 4,118 g1.DAT sprites decode
   to indices whose palette colours equal the existing RGBA decoder's. Why now (instead of an interim mask): the
   water blend must hit only what was drawn BEFORE the water tile, which needs in-order blending — what an 8-bit

3m. [x] **Zoom 1-3 in the CPU renderer (blocks making scene mode the default).** DONE ticks 168-173 (zoom 1-3 incl. masked images and zoom branches; vehicles in the sort). Upstream
   (Graphics/SoftwareDrawingContext.cpp drawImagePaletteSet + getDrawImagePosArgs, DrawSpriteBMP.hpp /
   DrawSpriteRLE.hpp, 248 lines): an element with hasZoomSprites (G1 flag 1<<4) at zoom>0 recurses at half
   coordinates into image - zoomOffset (rt x/y/w/h >>1, zoom-1); noZoomDraw (1<<5) skips at zoom>0; positions are
   aligned to the zoom grid (BMP masks y, RLE does not; RLE pre-shifts by ~zoomMask), then BMP samples every
   2^zoom pixel and RLE walks runs writing x>>zoom on rows i>>zoom. goloco has the G1 flags and ZoomOffset
   (pkg/assets) but gfx8.Sprite drops them and gfx8 only does zoom 0. Steps: (a) Sprite keeps flags+zoomOffset,
   port getDrawImagePosArgs+BMP/RLE zoom with tests (zoom 0 must stay byte-identical); (b) RenderTarget zoom +
   the recursion; (c) paint session/scene at zoom (viewport scale, culling, zoom-dependent branches:
   trees/shadows zoom<=1, snow zoom<=2, gridlines/station cargo zoom 0…); (d) world.drawScene for zoom>0.

## Completion measure: OpenLoco's clickables (Joop 2026-11-05: "a lot of OpenLoco clickables don't exist in
Goloco yet, probably a good proxy for completion as well")
To build: an inventory of every window's interactive widgets in OpenLoco (Ui/Windows/*.cpp: the widget lists
and what onMouseUp / onMouseDown / onDropdown / tool handlers do with each) against what goloco's windows
handle — a count per window and overall, kept in docs/STATUS.md and refreshed as windows are ported; the
missing ones become the window items' to-do lists. A script (scripts/clickables) so the count isn't by hand.
STATUS 2026-11-05 (later): markers in place — `// clickable(Window.cpp): names` where a widget works as upstream's,
`// clickable-partial(Window.cpp): names` where it does something but not yet what upstream does; the script
counts those (name matching dropped) and lists unannotated windows as such, not as zero. The label pattern now
takes camelCase names too (Vehicle.cpp's widx::buildNew …): 517 clickables over 47 windows. Annotated so far:
BuildVehicle 7 / 16, Vehicle 1 / 26, ToolbarTop 1 + 4 partial / 8, ToolbarTopCommon 1 + 2 partial / 6.
NEXT: annotate the rest of the ported windows (Options, the lists, ScenarioSelect, Construction, title, time
panel, prompts), then the figure into docs/STATUS.md.
EARLIER: scripts/clickables.py — upstream side done and solid: 429 clickables (the `case …widx::name:`
labels of each window's handlers) over 41 windows, the biggest LandscapeGeneration 52, CompanyWindow 49,
Options 46, Construction 40, ScenarioOptions 38. goloco side NOT trustworthy yet: matching widget names in
the ported files finds 32 / 429 (7%), a heavy undercount — goloco's ports often name widgets their own way
(BuildVehicle shows 0 / 15 though its close button and type tabs work; Options 1 / 46). NEXT: explicit
markers in the ports (`// clickable: Options.cpp show_fps`), counted by the script instead of name matching;
annotate the windows already ported, then put the real figure in docs/STATUS.md.

## Blocked
- (resolved 2026-11-05 ~18:45) the silent exit 1: not silent — the "audio: audio error: oto: ALSA error …" line
  IS log.Fatal's output: this VPS has no sound device, ebiten's RunGame returns the audio error, goloco quits.
  Headless runs need GOLOCO_MUTE=1. (Earlier "ALSA errors are only warnings" holds on Joop's machine, which has
  sound.) The real fix is item AU below.

- (RESOLVED 2026-11-01: CI green — workflow pushed over SSH, source checks fixed) GitHub CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml) had failed on every push (60/60): the runner lacks the
  X11/GL/ALSA headers Ebitengine builds against, and pkg/ui + pkg/world tests need a display. Fixed workflow
  waits in docs/ci.yml.pending (apt packages + xvfb-run, setup-go@v5 with go.mod's version). Pushing it needs
  the gh token's `workflow` scope (refused, as for Stelo's Pages workflow); disabling the workflow via
  `gh workflow disable` was refused by the permission check as a CI bypass. Joop: `gh auth refresh -h
  github.com -s workflow`, then `git mv docs/ci.yml.pending .github/workflows/ci.yml` and push (or ask me to).

- 2026-11-04 (Joop: 'The web version doesn't seem to cycle through or e.g. animate the water … so I don't see vehicles running') — FOUND: one Ebitengine update was one game tick (world.Update's tiles / waves, the title sequence) and the sim's budget assumed 60 updates a second; the web page draws ~7 frames a second (the CPU renderer ~100 ms a frame in wasm), and Ebitengine there runs one update per frame (measured: [Frames] '18 frames, 18 updates' — framestats now counts updates), so the whole game ran at about a tenth of its speed: the title waited a minute on each view, vehicles crawled. FIXED as upstream paces it (OpenLoco.cpp update / fixedUpdate / tick): World.GameTicks — tick() at most 40 times a second (25 ms accumulator, cap 500 ms), each tick() running numUpdates = time since the last / 31 ms, clamped 1–3, 1 when the mouse moved (hasPendingMouseInputUpdate) — and the title and play loops step world.Update, the sim tick and the title sequence that many times (× the speed in play; none when paused). The desktop now runs ~40 ticks a second (it ran 60 before). TestGameTicks (60 fps → ≈ 400 ticks in 10 s; 7 fps → 3 a frame; mouse moving → 1). Web check (headless Chromium, scratch build): the title cuts to its next view every 12–18 s (370 ticks ≈ 9–12 s upstream), vehicles at Kansas City move between shots. Still slow while the mouse moves on the web (upstream's 1-a-frame rule at a low frame rate — the frame rate is the real limit). FOUND too: water never animates — palette cycling (upstream's animated palette: water, lights) is not ported anywhere; queued as 5P-side item W. Also (Joop): the page's start panel has 'Show goloco's log in the corner' (off by default, remembered); the log drawer comes back on a panic / fatal error, a failed start or the game exiting.

- 2026-11-05 (FPS (2): the last dirty "miss" explained — overnight) — the checker now logs the view position with each difference (so GOLOCO_DIAG_PAINT_AT = view + screen pixel). The 132-px patch (view 528,7188, screen 724–740 × 264–279) is painted by the same four structs every time — surface 31119, building 14113 + child 14117, and a 1×1×1 "bridge"-typed struct with image 3631 — but in two different orders: the bridge struct after the building in 123 paint sessions, before it in 35. Not a goloco miss. - 2026-11-06 (the flaky glfw panic: chased) — (the other three wait on Joop → here.) cmd/goloco's only test (TestConfigValue) never touches glfw, so the panic comes from what the package links (Ebitengine wants a display as soon as it is linked in — GameViber split its window into a binary of its own for that). Rather than guess a fix: scripts/test-all.sh runs pkg/... and cmd/goloco each under its own xvfb-run (the combination that failed is no longer the routine) and keeps a failing run's full output (its stack) — the loop's test step uses it from now on. Looked for upstream's palette animation to port: there is none — Gfx.cpp loads palettes (and the intro fades them), SoftwareDrawingEngine::updatePalette is only called by those loads; water moves through WaveManager's waves, which goloco already ports and runs each tick (title included). - 2026-11-06 (W2: the waves were never drawn — fixed) — (the other three wait on Joop → here.) The waves diag (GOLOCO_DIAG_BUILDVEHICLE=waves: the view on the map's most open water) showed a lake whose frames 3 s apart differed in 0 pixels, though a probe found the live game running 3–6 waves. Three causes, all fixed: (1) the scene renderer — the live default — had "PARTIAL: no waves" in its water paint: PaintWater now attaches image + 60 + the wave's frame on a flag-6 tile at zoom 0 (PaintSurface.cpp), the frame through a Renderer.WaveFrame hook the world sets (it holds the wave table); (2) the column workers are clones made once and didn't get the hook — passed on each time now (found by TestWavesDrawn: 0 pixels before, 42 after); (3) UpdateWaves / createWave never invalidated the wave's tile, which upstream's WaveManager does on create and every update, so the dirty-region renderer never repainted it — both calls added. Now: the live lake's frames differ (63 pixels), a wave stroke visible; TestWavesDrawn: a wave changes 42 pixels, frame 3 → 9 changes 58. Joop's 2026-11-04 "water doesn't animate" was this. All tests green (scripts/test-all.sh); test page redeployed.
